## CHECKPOINT 2026-09-26 23:05 EDT -- RULE-BASED TRANSITIONAL CLASSIFIER FROM SEGMENTATIONS: a test on the whole CT corpus against the existing classifiers, with adjudication (Greg, verbatim intent below; workflow RULECLASS, id in the next block)

**Greg's rule set (2026-09-26 23:00):**
1. **Count.** Number of segmented vertebrae between the sacrum and the last rib-bearing vertebra: five is normal; six rib-free bodies flags either a sixth lumbar (L6) or a twelfth thoracic missing its rib (aplastic 12th rib). Local morphology of the sixth rib-free body separates the two (thoracic body/facet/transverse-process shape vs lumbar).
2. **Sacralization and lumbar ribs** (less confident): separated by the confusion between SPINEPS and TotalSegmentator, and by sacral morphology: if a completely sacralized L5 is absorbed into the sacrum label, the sacrum's morphology is abnormal (normal vs abnormal sacral shapes from the segmentations). Partial sacralization and lumbar ribs likewise from label disagreement plus shape.
3. **T13**: needs the whole rib cage (13 pairs), or the morphology of the 13th rib (more likely a stump rib) plus an odd-shaped vertebra: a stump rib with a strange body is a possible T13. Least confident.
4. **Why.** Correct, consistent identification of rare instances across the massive CT corpus -> synthetic X-rays (DRRs) of the rare classes -> better detection on real X-rays (the experiments may already show it weakly) -> if synthetic-to-fluoro transfers, the problem is solved.
5. **Ask.** Run this on the corpus as a test: does it agree with the existing classifiers, whatever they are; adjudicate the disagreements; report back.

**Existing classifiers/labels to compare against** (the workflow scouts exact paths): the anomaly registry `tools/lstv_flag_labels_v2.py` output (master; 749 anomalous spines in three tiers, memory anomaly-manifest-sources) and `tools/anomaly_manifest.py`; VerSe'20 Castellvi table and VerSe-native ids (25 = L6, 28 = T13) in the raw VerSeFusion masks (`VerSeFusion/data/unified`; the 813 renders drop C1-T9, and `has_T13` in the manifest is wrong: 18 claimed, 6 true); SPINEPS/VERIDAH T13/L6 calls (ids can be scrambled in pre-147428d harvests: guard `tools/spineps_ids.py`); TotalSegmentator (no L6 class: a sixth lumbar is named L5 with a one-level shift); CTSpinoPelvic1K's six-way taxonomy (filename/count heuristics) and its two-reader Castellvi consensus (`CTSpinoPelvic1K-1/docs/castellvi_consensus.csv`); COBRA screen (`tools/cobra_lstv_screen.py`); the R+C=22 rule (`docs/ANOMALY_RULE_EVALUATION.md`: arithmetic, computable on 1.2 %); the earlier plan `docs/CT_LSTV_CLASSIFIER_PLAN.md`; rib labels (trust-TS numbering, 13 ribs per side, lumbar ribs 60/61 in the v10 scheme; canonical rib triage `qc_v4_ribs_and_verts.py`); per-level morphometry from ostk (`OpenSpineToolkit/ostk/morphometry.py`; MDPA level atlas T11-L5; `docs/LEVEL_MORPHOMETRY.md` traps: a maximum extent is not a diameter, a first slice is not a plane).

5 pp (p0 0.926, n_min 48, zero errors: 56/56 -> Wilson LB 0.936 passes, 55/56 -> 0.906 fails), and the automated reference's own

**Beating LevelCheck's 97 %, honestly: SELECTIVE accuracy (LEVELCHECK_MODEL.md section 13, commit 2993f40,
20:58, before unblinding).** SchwingNet accepts a frame (anchored, no F1 disagreement flag, coupled margin >=
tau_s fixed on VALIDATION for >= 99.5 % validation accepted accuracy) or refers it to the surgeon. S1 = accepted-
person accuracy vs p0 0.976 (Manbachi), one-sided exact binomial, on ALL real-frame test persons (COBRA +
C-ArmLSpine), coverage ALWAYS printed beside it -- not the same quantity as LevelCheck's no-abstention 97.6 %.
Power (accepted persons): n_min 152 (all correct); 80 % power at 152-230 if accepted accuracy is 99.9 %, 299-363
at 99.5 %, 708-763 at 99.0 %; zero power below 152. F1b = flag at >= 97 % validation sensitivity. dual-loss-flag
relaunched (wf_9b0b95db-2b1, resumed) to fix tau/tau_s/F1b on validation and score S1 too. **The binding risk for
S1:** enough accepted real-frame test persons at >= 99.5 % -- COBRA naming has not been measured with the level
model (its sacral count was ~71 % on WV truth).

## History (superseded 21:15): checkpoint 2026-09-23 21:00 EDT

**The mechanism, pre-specified before unblinding (Greg: "surgeons get tricked by the count; a network has both the
count and the local morphology"; and "a dual loss using classification and count to flag anomalous spines, high
sensitivity").** LEVELCHECK_MODEL.md section 12, commit **1897772** (20:50 EDT; time-text fix in the next
commit), secondary analyses, H1-H6 untouched: **M1** COUNT (the surrogate for a surgeon's count from the sacrum)
vs MORPHOLOGY vs COUPLED on the same frames, exact one-sided McNemar on lumbosacral transitional test persons (T13
descriptive: a sacral count does not err there); **F1** the transitional flag = non-ordinary alphabet OR
count/morphology disagreement OR the dual-loss spine probability >= tau, tau fixed on VALIDATION for >= 95 %
sensitivity, target sensitivity lower bound >= 85 %; **D1** the dual-loss arm (level CE + alphabet term lambda
1.0 + spine-level transitional head). Development evidence already on disk: alphabet loss lambda 1.0 raised L6
recall 11-13 points (to 67-72 %) at +0.23 points false L6; the disagreement stop signal caught 4/5 error frames at
17/225 false flags, unflagged 921/922 correct. **Workflow dual-loss-flag wf_9b0b95db-2b1** builds head_dual.py

the loss, least squares compares two INDEPENDENT noise realisations, and smoothing always lowers that error -- the
constrained fits drove sigma_r UP to the box edge (3-4), alpha to its maximum 2.0, and n_e stayed 0: the refit
reinforced the very blur bias it was built to remove. Next: **workflow card-refit-v2**, separated objectives from
the detector-physics literature (IEC 62220-1-style: resolution from edge-spread functions, noise from the noise
power spectrum on homogeneous regions, deterministic terms on low-passed images), same acceptance gates.

### THE LEARNING CURVE, IN THE RIGHT CONVENTION (2026-09-23 10:40) -- the result reverses

`lcurve_score.py` had computed **all three** conventions for every cell and `lcurve_report.py` quoted whichever
the device had outline truth for; C-ArmLSpine has both, so its curve was reported in the retired whole-vertebra
convention. `tools/lcurve_lists.py` now sets the C-ArmLSpine convention to **body** (A28), and the report was
re-run (job 40417427). Nothing was retrained -- the body numbers were on disk the whole time.

**C-ArmLSpine (FIRST Surgio-B51, the intraoperative device), conf 0.74, vertebral-body boxes, 287 truth
vertebrae over 28 held-out cases, case-clustered intervals:**

| real frames of this C-arm in training | vertebra recall |
|---|---|
| stage 1 only (styled renders of the other two C-arms; this device never seen) | **12.5 %** [9.2, 15.9] |
| 50 (25 cases) | **81.5 %** [76.1, 86.8] |
| 100 | 81.5 % [76.3, 86.6] / 80.5 % (second seed) |
| 200 | 80.1 % [74.5, 85.7] / 82.2 % (second seed) |
| 210 (all real frames of this device) | 84.0 % [78.2, 89.2] |
| styled renders + all real frames | **85.0 %** [79.3, 90.3] |

At conf 0.25 the same rows read 15.0 %, 88.5 %, 87.8-88.9 %, 89.5 %, 89.9 %, 89.5 %. Sacrum recall goes
66.1 % -> 94.6 % at N = 50 and never moves again.

**What it says, and it is the experiment no DRR paper has run:** styled renders alone transfer poorly to an unseen
C-arm (12.5 %); **fifty real frames of that C-arm buy +69 points and reach 96 % of what every frame we hold
gives**; a hundred and two hundred add nothing beyond the seed noise. The earlier reading -- "fine-tuning halves
recall" -- was the box convention and nothing else. Every other detector number in the paper is still in the old
convention and must be re-read the same way before it is quoted (A28).

### THE CORPUS MANIFOLD LANDED ON THE GRID AND SAT UNCOLLECTED (found 2026-09-23 11:10)

Greg asked whether the resume was actually being worked. It was not, in one place: **the manifold / SVM study
finished on the grid between 03:37 and 08:00 today** -- after its workflow agent died on the model limit -- and
nothing had been collected. `/rs/.../or_ready/corpus_manifold/`: `MANIFOLD.md` (37 KB), `probes.json`,
`embedding.csv`, `deep.npy` (ResNet-50 avgpool, 2,048-d), four pairwise matrices. **15,154 frames, 38 sets**, two
feature spaces (37 image statistics; deep PCA-256), linear SVM and logistic, **leave-one-source-out**, clusters by
person / specimen / phantom / CT subject / acquisition day, 2,000 cluster-resamples. FluoroCap excluded throughout.

**(i) Film versus fluoroscopy.** In the hand-crafted statistics the boundary is NOT there: pooled
**63.4 %** against a **64.4 %** chance (SVM, leave-one-source-out) -- at or below chance, confirming the earlier
finding. In the deep space it IS there: **85.0 %** against the same 64.4 % chance. So film and fluoroscopy differ
in a way nine physics terms do not capture.

**(ii) Source identity.** Pairwise separability is ~1.00 for almost every pair of the 19 probed sources -- every
corpus is trivially identifiable from its pixels. The informative exceptions are the pairs that SHOULD resemble
each other: TotalSegmentator vs VerSe DRRs **0.823**, CTSpinoPelvic1K vs VerSe DRRs 0.954, **BUU-LSPINE vs
Mendeley 0.919** (independent support for excluding Mendeley as a BUU redistribution), COBRA Elekta vs Varian
0.983.

**(iii) THE TRANSFER TEST, and it is the important one.** Per device, case-grouped folds, the same CT subject
never on both sides: **styled renders against real frames of that same device separate at 99.9-100.0 %** in both
spaces and both classifiers (Cios Fusion: stats/SVM 99.9 % [99.5, 100.0], deep/SVM 100.0 %). Vanilla renders
against real: 99.9-100.0 %. Vanilla against styled: 97.6-100.0 % -- so **the styling measurably changes the image
but does not move it toward the real frame.** The typicality-gated corpora behave identically.

**What this means, stated plainly:** the physics parameterisation does not make a render pass for a real frame of
that device, and the manifold says why the learning curve looks as it does -- a detector trained on styled
renders alone is operating out of distribution, which is the 12.5 % on the unseen C-arm. The paper cannot claim
transfer is solved; it can claim the gap is measured in three independent ways (a linear probe at ~100 %, a
detector at 12.5 %, and a fifty-frame price to fix it).

Files pulled to `SchwingNet/results/manifold/`; `dc_P_pooled/scores.json` (B1, the pooled all-device detector,
written 01:50) was also uncollected and is now at `SchwingNet/results/dc_P_pooled_scores.json`.

### A32. THE CARD REFIT (submitted 2026-09-23 12:10, array 40418162)

Greg: *"do all that parameter refitting then with the new terms as well."* First, a correction to what I told him:
**the model already has penumbra (`sigma_p`) and a collimator field**; the "NO TERM" labels in the first
`FORMATION_GAP.md` came from a mapping I wrote, not from the model. Their fitted values are wrong, which is a
different problem and a smaller one.

**THE ROOT CAUSE, and it is methodological.** `device_fit.sse_sst` calls `forward(th, d, RP)` with `noisy=None`:
a NOISELESS model is fitted by least squares to a NOISY frame. Least squares against noise always prefers blur --
every extra millimetre of `sigma_r` removes variance the model cannot explain -- and `alpha` then rises to put back
the edges the blur ate. The three measured defects are three readings of that one cause: receptor blur too strong
(edge sharpness styled 3.58 vs real 1.55 px on Cios, 5.25 vs 2.19 on Elekta), edge gain too high (overshoot 0.473
vs 0.036 on Surgio), high-frequency noise power fifteen times too low (real nps4 0.0356 vs styled 0.0023).

**THE REFIT** (`tools/device_refit.py`, `slurm/device_refit.sh`, array 40418162 over Cios / Surgio / Elekta):
- the quantum draw is INSIDE the loss, at the card's fitted photon count, with a fixed per-frame seed;
- the loss gains `device_physics.stat_distance` between the model's and the real frame's image statistics --
  radial noise-power spectrum, edge-halo profile, gradient quantiles -- combined by stated weights (`STAT_W`,
  nps and halo weighted highest, because they are the defects);
- two new terms after the receptor blur, where a detector adds them so they are not smoothed away: `n_e`
  (electronic / structured noise amplitude) and `n_beta` (its 1/f^beta slope). At `n_e = 0` the model is exactly
  the old one;
- the optimiser STARTS FROM THE PUBLISHED CARD, so the refit can only improve the combined loss, and the old card
  is scored under the same noisy statistic-aware loss on held-out frames so the two rows are comparable;
- nothing overwrites `docs/devices/<dev>.json`: the refit lands in `or_ready/devices_refit/<dev>.refit.json` and is
  adopted only if it wins on BOTH r2 and stat_distance and then lowers the styled-vs-real separability.

ACCEPTANCE, in order: (1) held-out r2 not worse and stat_distance lower; (2) re-render a styled set with the refit
card and re-run `tools/formation_gap.py` -- the nps / edge_sigma / overshoot effect sizes must collapse; (3) re-run
the styled-vs-real probe -- it is currently ~100 % and must fall. Only then do the cards, the corpus and every
styled number move.

Two smoke-test findings already fixed and worth keeping: `stat_distance` returns a DICT of components on different
scales (a `float()` on it raised, and a bare `except` was swallowing the whole statistic term -- it read 0.0); and
the published fitted medians live at `card["physics_terms"]["fitted"]`, not `card["fitted"]`.

### The audit's finding, in one line

**99 gaps: 28 bugs, 21 unfinished, 4 budget cuts, 15 unknown, 31 with a written criterion.** Worst first in
`docs/DATA_COMPLETENESS_AUDIT.md`. The ones that change the science:

- **The Elekta device card -- the physics parameterisation -- was fitted on 40 frames from THREE cases** (smoke
  scale; 149 of 152 fetched cases never used in the fit). Refit on the full 692 after the fetch.
- **COBRA Varian (225 cases) is unusable today**: `export_case` returns "Varian geometry not solved (out of
  scope)"; the patient-to-RTK axis map was only solved for Elekta. Solve it or state it.
- **A detector arm trained on 5,013 duplicate COBRA frames** (derived banks cobra_elekta_v2/_ap45 were globbed
  into one arm's training and 338 into its validation) -- a leak, not a shortfall.
- **C-ArmLSpine case134's AP and lateral DO exist**, as extension-less XA files inside `case134/CBCT/`; the
  builder walked only `C-arm/`. And **case118's "lateral" is an oblique at -58.1 deg** (the OBL view class landed
  in commit 0b0424d at 21:20, after the 20:24 frames build). Rebuild both.
- **CT 0533 is truncated in the PUBLIC release** (the same bytes on HF), not just on the grid; and **the HF mirror
  still carries v10 labels** (the withdrawn S1 carve) eleven days after v11.
- **VerSe: 10 of 319 VerSe'20 series were never downloaded**; `verse_soft_drr` carries the row-spacing defect on
  181 of 374; `ctsp_drr_obl/obl2` (3,767 renders) were rendered from the wrong tree with C1-T9 stripped.
- **TotalSegmentator: SPINEPS produced no mask for 110 of 1,228 v2 CTs** (72 of them whole spines, never
  diagnosed), and the bench was never extended to v3.
- **NHANES II: the release is 17,075 entries and all but 2 are on disk** (the CDC serves those two as 0 bytes);
  only 543 annotated films were ever used -- D2 says use them all.
- **VinDr's three counts are three different objects**: 744 = films of the old lateral-only route, 2,632 = crops
  in the current bank, 10,274 = pseudo-labelled renders. Fix the wording everywhere; all 10,466 images are on disk.
- **Dataset815 (1,176 cases, the L6 set) was never consumed**: both prep jobs died on a missing singularity path.
- **SpineSAC has no bone truth**: TotalSegmentator returned empty masks on all three sawbone CTs ("Crop is empty").

## THE FINAL BANK'S DESIGN PARAMETERS, from the deep research (reports/Synthetic real ratio X-ray detectors.md, 119 citations)

No DRR/DeepDRR paper has ever swept the synthetic:real ratio or the real-frame count (all train synthetic-only,
test real). The measured window comes from adjacent sweeps and is consistent across four modalities:

| parameter | value | rests on |
|---|---|---|
| scheme | two stages: synthetic-only pretraining on the whole render bank under STRONG domain randomisation, then FULL-DEPTH fine-tuning (no frozen layers) on a synthetic+real mixture | two-stage beat joint at 160 real; n.s. at 2,727; freezing cost up to 13.5 pct |
| ratio in the fine-tune mix | real frames 10-20 pct of sampled images (synthetic 4:1 to 9:1); never beyond 10:1 | YOLOv3 5-20 pct real optimum; RoentGen 1:1 > 5:1; Moroianu plateau 5:1; Sagers/Tang saturate 10-11:1 |
| pretraining size | the whole bank, unbounded by ratio | 565k synthetic then <10k real matched 66k real |
| rare-class oversampling of REAL frames | 4x epoch-level repetition of real L6/T13 frames; NO parity rebalancing; report rare recall WITH PR-AUC and common-class precision at a fixed operating point | VERIDAH 4x -> 96-97 pct recall, 99.18 pct scans correct; Dushenev recall peaks at 4x while PR-AUC falls; ISIC parity -> nevus 0.35 |
| rare-class share of the render bank | 25-35 pct of rendered CTs carry the variant, by CHOOSING WHICH CTs TO RENDER, not by duplicating renders; synthetic per real rare frame capped near 10:1 | VerSe'20 L6 in 85 of ~253 junction-visible scans (~34 pct) moved L6 from 0 methods to almost all; T13 with 6 scans never stabilised |
| distinct CTs vs renders per CT | distinct CTs first (dozens minimum for the variant: 28 L6 training scans sufficed, 6 T13 did not); 200-500 renders per CT | SyntheX flattened at 250-500 renders/CT; CT count never ablated anywhere |
| real frames per device | 300-500 labelled, >= 100 per view floor | Barr curve; Gherardini 240 frames; carpal YOLOv5s ~100 |
| fidelity | physics renders + strong randomisation; no CycleGAN stage | SyntheX 9.05 vs 20.55 mm; CycleGAN adds nothing at scale; RadRotator content alteration |
| curriculum / weighting | none measured in X-ray; keep mosaic/mixup; avoid weighted BCE for the rare class | COCO-ZIPF |

The experiment nobody has published and this project can run: a REAL-FRAME LEARNING CURVE per device
(50 / 100 / 200 / 400 real frames on top of the render pretraining) with the transitional class scored separately,
and distinct CTs varied against renders per CT. Launched as workflow real-frame-learning-curve (detector side,
on the existing devcorpus renders + real frames; no final bank needed). The classifier-side ablation (distinct
transitional CTs vs renders per CT) runs on the final bank.

Manuscript habits from the evidence: label every ratio by scheme (joint mix vs pretrain-then-fine-tune); report
rare recall beside PR-AUC and common precision; state in as many words that no DRR study swept the ratio.

The full checklist and every finding follow below; the sections are chronological.

      them.** The overlap row measured mean centroid-to-centroid lumbar pitch of **31.7 mm** on verse108 (T12→L5
      span 158.4 mm at `mm_per_px_row_si` = 1.00). Published adult lumbar centre-to-centre is ~36–38 mm per level,
      ~180–190 mm over T12–L5. That is ~15 % short, and the known cause of exactly this is the **DRR row-spacing
      defect** (A-P spacing written into the S-I row field, found 2026-09-19). `~/verse_drr/ann` is dated
      2026-09-19 12:57, which **straddles the fix**. Every millimetre this row would print depends on that field.
      Re-render one case post-`sweep_v3` and compare, before any millimetre from `verse_drr` reaches the paper.
      This is the project's own rule working: the number looked reasonable and only failed against a value from
      outside the computation.

- [~] **A27. Morphology synthesis — THE EVIDENCE IS IN, THE DECISION IS GREG'S** (workflow
      `spine-synthesis-prior-art`, wx5ofwdvf, relaunched wi9v9ibri). The bespoke warp Greg rejected
      (workflow wmftddyvx, STOPPED) is not what is proposed any more. Six angles searched and the top
      candidates verified against the papers themselves: `docs/MORPHOLOGY_SYNTHESIS_PRIOR_ART.md`, with
      the evidence files in `research_notes/spine synthesis prior art/`. **Nothing is implemented.**
      **THE FINDING: no published method meets the requirement, and nobody has ever synthesised a
      transitional vertebra — no L6, no T13, no sacralised L5, in any modality, on any anatomy named by
      its position in a chain.** A diffeomorphism cannot change the label set; a fixed-length shape model
      cannot host an extra vertebra; the one mask-conditioned generator that was handed anatomy no patient
      has (seg2med) painted something that stopped being anatomy (FSIM 0.45 vs 0.82); and the only group
      that ran generated-CT → DRR → real-fluoroscopy end to end, on DeepFluoro which we hold, found that
      route the WORST of four (8.00 mm vs 4.38 mm landmark error).
      **WHAT THE DOCUMENT RECOMMENDS INSTEAD, in order:** (1) keep the control condition — affine +
      intensity is what every spine challenge winner, TotalSegmentator, SPINEPS and VERIDAH use, and
      nnU-Net removed elastic deformation on purpose; (2) for the transitional scarcity the only measured
      lever is real rare cases oversampled (VERIDAH ablation: thoracic anomaly recall 87.80 % → 32.93 %
      T2w and 96.30 % → 25.93 % CT when the labelled anomalies are removed); (3) if anatomical variation
      is wanted at all, adopt ONE published mechanism — PolyPose (NeurIPS 2025, arXiv:2505.19256,
      `github.com/eigenvivek/polypose`, **MIT**), validated on DeepFluoro at pelvis Dice 0.99, femurs
      0.98, HD95 1.48/1.56 mm and **0.00 % folds** — bounded by Panjabi's segmental ROM and reported as a
      measured, possibly negative augmentation experiment, because nobody has ever run one (PolyPose has
      zero downstream numbers in any paper).
      **THE DECISION GREG MUST MAKE**, and only he makes it: **do we spend 2–3 weeks on the bounded
      poly-rigid augmenter at all**, knowing it (a) cannot make an L6, (b) buys at best the 1–2 Dice
      points or ~5 % more findings that every honest measured result in this literature reports, and
      (c) would be the first such experiment published on spine, positive or negative — or do we spend
      the same weeks on more real transitional cases, which is the only thing the literature has measured
      working? If the answer is the augmenter, the plan in §4 is gated: reproduce their DeepFluoro numbers
      first (2 days, our own cohort) and stop if they do not come back; then per-level mask adherence
      ≥ ~0.95, a rendered DRR that is not broken at the end plates, a distribution check with SPREAD
      against published normative series, and a paired bootstrap A/B on REAL C-arm frames in the A28 body-box
      convention. Abandon at the first gate that fails; each is cheaper than the next.
      Resolved in passing and worth keeping: POLAFFINI is Apache-2.0, PolyPose is MIT, Meng's whole-spine
      SSM is non-commercial (blocks the commercialization track), NVIDIA's Open Model Licence terms are
      unread. The bespoke part, stated plainly: using the published transform FORWARD as a generator is
      the one step no paper has done.

## What was found today, with the file that proves it

**The synthetic corpus was not drawn from the distribution the cards learned.** `device_style.SPREAD = 0.2` clipped
every per-frame term to its central 60 %. Two defects, both measured, both in `docs/DEVICE_SPREAD_SWEEP.md`
(card-only, so it reproduces from any commit):

- It is a MARGINAL fix for a JOINT problem, applied to 8–11 correlated terms at once. A styled frame reaches
  **1.7 % (varian_halcyon) to 5.1 % (bv_pulsera)** of its device's fitted joint distribution.
- `np.clip` SATURATES, not truncates: an out-of-band draw is pinned to the boundary VALUE. **94.9–98.3 % of styled
  frames carry at least one pinned term**, 3.2–4.4 of 8–11 terms on average.

The second one was only visible in the figure — the clipped kVp histogram is stacked spikes, not a narrowed
distribution. Second time in this project that rendering settled what the numbers did not.

**The replacement is implemented but NOT adopted.** `device_style.SAMPLE_MODE = "typicality"` draws from the full
copula and rejects only draws whose Mahalanobis distance in copula normal scores exceeds the card's own 99th
percentile — the statistic `device_fit.coverage` already validates each card against on held-out frames. Marginals
intact, 99 % of the joint reachable. `tools/test_device_gate.py` asserts it and passes on all six cards. **The clip
is still the default.** The A/B that decides it is job 40415020's corpora plus a matched detector run; do not switch
the default on the argument alone.

**Gate in normal-score space, never through the quantile table.** The copula maps scores to values through a
41-point piecewise-linear inverse CDF; where a term is discrete (four mAs settings, a C-arm at AP and LAT) that
inverse is not unique. The first gate read values back through it and shifted a marginal median by 0.55 SD; the
first test measured typicality the same biased way and reported 88 % for a gate that was at 99.8 %.

**Cios Fusion kVp is on disk and unused.** The card says "not recorded in this dataset" because the 366 registered
DeepFluoro frames dropped their technique tags. The same acquisitions' vendor DICOMs carry kVp on 2,654 frames
across 84 values, mA across 14. ONE dataset in two representations — never count them as two. Recovering it needs a
per-frame join from vendor DICOM to H5 frame; the identity audit was asked whether that join is possible.

**The CT axis was too narrow.** `docs/CT_INVENTORY.md` (from `tools/ct_inventory.py`, keyed the same way the split
is) — ts_drr_v3 1,735, physics_drr 3,029, ts_drr 1,170, ctsp_drr 801, verse_drr 374, carm_drr 120, physics_rare 81,
spinalai_drr 44. NOT additive across banks. `docs/BENCHMARK_PROTOCOL.md` axis 3 rewritten.

**Pooled ≠ LODO.** `--design P` pools every device and tests on every device's real TEST split (disjoint by
SPECIMEN); LODO holds a device out entirely. Different questions; report both, never the pooled number alone.
The list builder REFUSES to write lists if a CT subject or a real specimen falls on two sides. First build: 18,906
train entries, 1,998 val, 17 held CT subjects, 56 test specimens. geneva_hip is test-only so it also scores as a
completely unseen device for free.

## The open thing, and why it matters

`tools/patient_keys.py` matches FILE NAMES with regexes and reads three ann fields. Greg's objection, and he is
right: the identity is in the metadata. Concretely, already confirmed by reading the anns:

- `fluorocap_drr` carries `panel_path = panels/commons/<article-slug>__a.png`. Panels `__a` and `__b` of one figure
  are ONE patient. The keyer never looked at this field. 2,385 anns.
- `spinalai_drr` carries `patient_id = sim_000237` — 8,358 renders over ~44 patients.
- `aasce_drr` case `AASCE_sunhl-1th-<date>-<n>_<A|B>_AP`; A/B are one patient's two films. 457 anns.
- `anchor_drr` case `ctsp_0001_ap` — the CTSP release id, plainly there; the regex just demanded a `_p<k>` suffix.
- `ts_drr` carries `age`, `gender`, `study_type`, `ts_split`, `pathology`.

**The biggest unanswered question**: CTSpinoPelvic1K is built from CT Colonography, which scans each patient BOTH
PRONE AND SUPINE. If two release ids are one human being, keying on the release id is not keying on the patient and
the whole corpus is mis-split — worse than either leak already found. A project note says "351 separate-mode pelvic
acquisitions held out by design", which suggests someone knew. The audit workflow was asked to settle it. **Do not
assume it is fine.**

Also open: does TotalSegmentator v3 `s0001` denote the same patient as v2 `s0001`? If yes, ts_drr in training and
ts_drr_v3 in validation leaks the whole overlap.

## Where the numbers for the manuscript are

`docs/RSNAI_CLAIMS.json` — 54 claims, every one verified against a file on disk (`checked: true`). Headlines:

- real intraop C-arm, five-fold CV over 141 cases / 617 vertebrae: lateral whole frame **98.22 %** exact
  (95 % CI lower 95.71 %), 100 % within one; AP **96.67 %**; five in field 97.00 %; three 92.86 %; one 72.85 %
- **zero-shot** (no C-ArmLSpine training at all): lateral **96.27 %**, AP 37.88 %
- plain film naming: CSXA cervical **99.87 %** (n=3,720), BUU lumbar **98.04 %** (n=2,698), three visible 94.89 %
- detection, LODO with C-ArmLSpine held out: vertebra recall **90.59 %** (outline truth, IoU 0.5, conf 0.25),
  sacrum 83.93 %; under BODY truth 5.23 %, which is the convention mismatch, not a failure
- render-trained detector on real film **71.67 %** vs film-trained **99.14 %** — the sim-to-real tax
- film vs fluoroscopy, leave one corpus out: 71.11 % against 71.80 % chance — the modality boundary does not transfer

## Traps re-learned today

- `${VAR:-"a b c"}` — quoting inside the default protects it from word splitting, so the loop runs ONCE on a word
  made of the first and last entries. It silently styled one wrong pair. Use an array.
- Python's `write_text` on Windows turns `\n` into `\r\n`. rsync to the grid was fine, but check.
- A "drop blank lines" filter in a patch script strips EVERY blank line in the file. Restore from the grid copy.
- Bash-tool heredocs collapse backslashes: write patch scripts with the Write tool and run them by path.

## Two multi-agent workflows are in flight. Collect both.

**`wf_cffe41e5-5ab` — patient identity audit.** Seven corpora in parallel, each claim then adversarially verified.
Returns a specification to save as `docs/PATIENT_IDENTITY_SPEC.md`, then rewrite `tools/patient_keys.py`
metadata-first and extend `tools/test_patient_keys.py`. Transcript:
`.../subagents/workflows/wf_cffe41e5-5ab/journal.jsonl`.

Greg's objection, which is correct and is the brief: *"why can't you use metadata from the NIfTIs or the paper or
some other metadata to key these"*. The keyer regexes FILE NAMES and reads three ann fields. The identity was in
the metadata the whole time. Already confirmed by reading the anns:

| bank | the identity field that was ignored |
|---|---|
| `fluorocap_drr` (2,385) | `panel_path = panels/commons/<article-slug>__a.png` — panels `__a` and `__b` of one figure are ONE patient |
| `spinalai_drr` (8,358) | `patient_id = sim_000237` — ~44 patients, this one WAS read |

**The biggest open question, unchanged and still not settled: is a CTSpinoPelvic1K release id one person?** The
source is CT colonography, which scans each patient prone AND supine. If two ids are one human being the whole
corpus is mis-split. Do not assume it is fine. Second: does TotalSegmentator v3 `s0001` mean the same patient as
v2 `s0001`? If yes, ts_drr in training and ts_drr_v3 in validation leaks the entire overlap.

**`wf_5b61f666-397` — Figure 1 rebuilt to Greg's spec.** Five rows, one agent each, each renders AND opens its own
PNG, then a second agent opens the PNG and tries to find what is wrong with it, then a compose step. New tools:

### Greg's figure spec, in full, because it must not be lost

- **Row 2 — anatomy.** A cartoon lateral spine in the PRONE position, sacrum through C2, ending at the skull, with
  anatomically correct lumbar lordosis, thoracic kyphosis and cervical lordosis. Legible labels in the existing
  two-overlapping-boxes cartoon convention. The sacrum drawn and marked as THE ANCHOR. The algorithm shown in TWO
  STATES: left = initial, window on L2–L5; right = the k-th state, window on the middle four vertebrae, with a
  series of overlapping rectangles collated LIKE THE PAGES OF A BOOK behind it standing for the k−1 prior
  iterations — the ordinary computer-science convention for state *i* and state *i+1*. Render BOTH a typical VerSe
  spine and an anomalous one, preferably one carrying a T13, to show the algebraic rule bite.
- **Row 3 — overlap and alias.** Not a random assortment of crops. The same two states, on the SAME vertebrae as
  row 2, in the same box style, with the OVERLAPPING vertebrae shaded in both crops to show directly what the
  matcher stitches on. Measurement lines relating the C-arm window TRANSLATION to the average vertebral body
  width, in millimetres from the real data, because an alias here is an off-by-one and an off-by-one is
  wrong-level surgery.
- **Row 4 — fluoroscopic localisation.** Move it out of the plain-film domain. Show the limited fluoroscopic field
  of view being hashed/mapped onto the panoramic map, on a synthetic VerSe full spine carrying an LSTV or TLTV,
  targeting the middle-most vertebra. Include a monospaced readout of EXACTLY what the software would say: the
  *i*-th vertebra cranial-to-caudal, the *j*-th caudal-to-cranial, and the algebraic anomaly flag.
- **Row 5 — corpus as pictograms.** The old dataset panel "looks bad". Replace with drawn boxes: a CT pictogram
  with an ARROW to an X-ray for paired sets; an X-ray alone for pure radiograph sets; a stick-figure spine for
  phantoms; and a clear visual marker that the non-spine C-arm data contributes DEVICE SETTINGS ONLY, no anatomy.
- **Row 6 — architectures in AI-literature convention.** Blocks whose size encodes feature-map resolution, channel
  counts, skip connections as arcs — U-Net/ResNet/YOLO style, not a flowchart. One for the DETECTOR (1024 px,
  CSP backbone, PAN over three scales, decoupled head, classes vertebra / sacrum / C2) and one for the LOCALISER
  (96 px crop per body plus target-marker channel, ResNet-18 encoder, BiGRU over 2–9 bodies, posterior over 27
  levels).
- **The panel Greg asked for by name and it was already in the repo**: `paper/rsnai/figures/fig1_problem.png` —
  the grey stacked cartoon vertebrae with the red dashed sliding field-of-view box, showing that the same window
  holds L4·L3·L2 in an ordinary spine and L5·L4·L3 in a lumbarised one. It is now panel (a).
- **The algebraic rule**, stated once so it is not re-derived: C1 is always excluded, so a normal spine has 23
  vertebrae (C2–C7, T1–T12, L1–L5). For a target vertebra, R = count rostral, C = count caudal. Normal R + C = 22;
  anomalous otherwise. Worked anomalous example: target L1 on a spine with an L6 gives R = 18, C = 5, sum 23,
  flagged. C1 is drawn ghosted/crossed-out to show it is excluded from the arithmetic. An interactive version of
  this rule is already published as an artifact.

## Shareable pages

- **Figure gallery, every PNG on disk at full resolution with what each shows** (republished 21:10 under the current account — the signed-in account changed mid-session and the first link, JSHksQ6Tgad2w8cwJmaDZT, belongs to the previous one; the results page WSW2UJAR4ar2PjmbymLVRf has the same problem and needs republishing if wanted):
  https://claude.ai/artifact/XyZ6krXFYezkdkpj2kAKPw (private; share from the page menu). Source files are in
  paper/rsnai/figures/ and docs/figures/; the manuscript copies its set into SchwingNet/figures/.
- **Results page** (below).

### Results page

`https://claude.ai/artifact/WSW2UJAR4ar2PjmbymLVRf` — the figure, the verified numbers, and the honest statement
of where the method is behind. **Private**: Greg must share it from the page's Share menu. Republish by passing
that URL as `url`.

## The literature answer, from `docs/RSNAI_CLAIMS.json`, not from memory

**Ahead on naming.** Real intraoperative C-arm, five-fold CV over 141 cases / 617 vertebrae: lateral whole frame
**98.22 %** exact (95 % case-bootstrap CI lower 95.71 %), 100 % within one; AP **96.67 %**; five in field 97.00 %;
three 92.86 %; one 72.85 % (within one 90.23 %). **Zero-shot**, with no data from this collection in training:
lateral **96.27 %**, AP 37.88 %. Plain film: CSXA cervical **99.87 %** (n=3,720), BUU lumbar **98.04 %**
(n=2,698), three visible 94.89 %. The best published registration-free naming is 73.3 % and it imports identity
from a preoperative radiograph; LevelCheck registers to the patient's own CT and is a ceiling, not a rival.

**Behind on detection, which is the binding constraint.** Device held out entirely: per-body recall **90.59 %**
(outline truth, IoU 0.5, conf 0.25), sacrum 83.93 %. Published class-agnostic C-arm detection is 0.96–0.98, and
recall compounds over 8–12 bodies, so 90.6 % is not counting-grade. Render-trained detector on real film
**71.67 %** against film-trained **99.14 %** — the ~28-point sim-to-real tax, which matches the field. The same
predictions score 5.23 % against vertebral-BODY truth: a box-convention mismatch, not a failure, and reported
because a reader seeing only 90.6 % could not know the convention mattered.

## Also landed

`README.md` rewritten to explain the audit machinery — claims bound to source files, one split over people that
refuses rather than warns, content-addressed data accounting proved on a corpus with a PLANTED leak, cluster
bootstrap, the pre-registered benchmark protocol, the two device designs, the fitted-physics audit, and the
standing rule to look at the image. Figure 1 is embedded in it.

Workflow `w0wxw7kub` completed: the data-accounting chain now runs end to end on the real CLIs, with
`tools/diagnostics/make_fake_corpus.py` building a leaking and a clean synthetic corpus so the leak detector is
itself tested. `docs/DATA_ACCOUNTING.md` corrected — it had named output files no tool produces.

# THE ALGEBRAIC ANOMALY RULE WAS EVALUATED, AND IT DOES NOT HOLD UP

Workflow `wo6fp7c28` finished after the addendum above was written. `docs/ANOMALY_RULE_EVALUATION.md` and
`docs/anomaly_rule_evaluation.json` carry it; grid job 40414996; raw-mask inventory
`~/model_night/verse_mask_levels.json`. **Read this before drawing the rule into any figure or writing it into the
manuscript.** The figure workflow `wf_5b61f666-397` was launched with the rule's premise stated as fact and will
need correcting.

## 1. The rule has no target. It is a single count.

R + C = N − 1 for *every* choice of target, so "R + C ≠ 22" is exactly "N ≠ 23". Choosing a target vertebra is
decorative; the whole content is one count over the entire presacral column. Any figure implying the target
matters is misleading.

## 2. It needs the whole column, so it almost never applies.

C2 in the field AND the column reaching the lumbosacral junction AND no unlabelled body inside the run:

| corpus | spines | C2 present | reaches sacrum | **computable** |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| VerSe | 374 | 76 (20.3 %) | 239 (63.9 %) | **14 (3.7 %)** |
| CTSpinoPelvic1K | 801 | 0 | — | **0 (0.0 %)** |
| both | 1,175 | — | — | **14 (1.2 %)** |

CTSP is zero by construction, not by oversight: the spines are abdominopelvic (field starts about T10) and the
Dataset813 scheme has no cervical class. And the anomalies are mostly out of reach — 74 transitional spines exist
(VerSe 50 L6 + 6 T13, CTSP 18 L6) and **4 of 74 (5.4 %)** sit in a computable field.

## 3. The C1 premise is FALSE for VerSe, and the rule then flags every normal spine.

"C1 is always excluded from these datasets" is not true here: **C1 is labelled in all 76 VerSe cases that carry
C2** (89 of 89 in the renders). A detector that boxes C1 counts 24 on an ordinary column, and against the stated
constant of 22 the rule fires on everything: TP 4, FN 1, FP 9, TN 0 — sensitivity 0.800, **specificity 0.000**.
The constant belongs to the COUNTING CONVENTION (23 excluding C1, 24 including it), not to anatomy, and must be
set from what the detector actually puts a node on.

## 4. The 1.00 / 1.00 on the fourteen is circular, not evidence.

TP 5, FN 0, FP 0, TN 9 — sensitivity 1.000 (Wilson 0.566–1.000), specificity 1.000 (0.701–1.000). With an exact
count, the rule and the definition of "anomalous" are the same sentence; only a count-preserving anomaly could
disagree and there was none. What that row establishes is the size of the test set: **five anomalous spines.**

## 5. It is not robust to the detector it would run on.

Exact binomial convolution, checked against 200,000 Monte Carlo draws (agree within 0.27 pts). With per-body miss
rate p and spurious-node rate q, false alarm on a normal spine: p=0.01,q=0 → 20.6 %; p=0.02 → 37.2 %; p=0.05 →
69.3 %. Missed extra vertebra (N=24): p=0.02,q=0 → 30.2 %.

**Inverted, the number a reviewer will press on:** holding the false alarm at 5 % needs **p ≤ 0.223 %** — the chain
must come out complete on 95 % of whole columns. 1 % false alarm needs p ≤ 0.044 %. Measured chain completeness
(`docs/SWEEP_PROTOCOL.md`) is 95 % on 203 C2-anchored TotalSegmentator scans, which implies about a 5 % false-alarm
rate before any spurious node is counted — and that is on rendered CT, not real intraoperative frames. Misses were
modelled as independent; the measured failures cluster by region, so independence is the convenient case, not the
safe one.

## 6. Three structural blind spots.

- **It localises nothing.** 5 of 5 computable anomalous spines flagged, 0 localised. N=24 holds three T13 spines
  and one L6 spine, and nothing in R + C separates them — which is precisely the question at the lumbosacral
  junction. 100 % flagged-but-not-localisable, by construction.
- **Transpositions pass.** R + C is invariant under any permutation of the names, so a chain with swapped names
  satisfies the rule exactly.
- **An extra and a missing vertebra cancel**, and the column reads normal.

## 7. What to do with it

Keep it as a cheap *sanity flag* where the whole column is in view, with the constant set from the detector's own
counting convention and the false-alarm rate quoted beside it. Do not present it as an anomaly detector, do not
imply it localises, and do not draw it with a target vertebra as though the target mattered. The honest line is
that it applies to 1.2 % of the spines here and cannot tell a T13 from an L6.

## Two incidental findings, both real

- **`~/verse_drr` holds 387 files for 374 spines: 13 EXACT DUPLICATE renders.** `glgl003` … `glgl146` repeat
  `gl003` … `gl146` with identical shape and identical boxes. That is the doubled-prefix naming bug, and it is a
  same-person-twice case — hand it to the identity audit (`wf_cffe41e5-5ab`).
- **`CTSP_COLONOG_730`** has levels named T10, T11, T12, L1, L2, **L4**, L5 with an ordinary centre-to-centre step
  (0.91 of its neighbours) between the bodies named L2 and L4 — rendered and looked at. Either that patient has
  four lumbar bodies named as five, or the name L3 was skipped.

## A method worth keeping

A **box gap cannot find a missing body**: posterior elements overhang the body below, so neighbouring boxes always
overlap and every observed gap in 387 renders was negative. The working test is centre-to-centre spacing, each step
compared against its own neighbours — p99 of the step ratio is 1.17, a skipped body sits near 2.0, threshold 1.6.
Two of 374 trip it: `verse753` (a 12-voxel T9 speck the renderer dropped under MIN_PIX=60) and `verse097` (a
sharply angulated cervicothoracic junction, where a step measured along one axis is not a step along the column —
a limitation of the test, recorded as one).

**Truth source note:** this was computed from the raw VerSe label volumes (`VerSeFusion/data/unified`, VerSe
numbering, 25=L6, 28=T13), NOT from the render annotations, because the renderer drops silhouettes under 60 px.
That mattered in 1 of 374 cases. Cross-checks all held: T13 = 6 VerSe cases (against the VerSeFusion manifest's
wrong 18), CTSP L6 = 18 (matches `slurm/l6_cases.txt` exactly).

# RIGOR AUDIT (workflow `w1niwx23d`) — what a reviewer would find, ranked

17 agents, adversarially verified. Full result:
`.../tasks/w1niwx23d.output`; per-agent journal at `.../subagents/workflows/wf_7c8a7106-128/journal.jsonl`.
**Fix these before the manuscript goes anywhere.** They are ordered by how much damage each does if a reviewer
finds it first.

## 1. Selective reporting — the one that would actually hurt

Two places where many cells were computed and one was reported:

- `or_ready/lodo/rescore_outline.md` scans **6 detector arms × 3 sets × 16 confidence thresholds × 3 box
  conventions**. The manuscript reports one cell.
- `corpus_manifold` computes the leave-one-corpus-out test in **three feature spaces**. The manuscript reports the
  only one that falls **below chance** — which is the one that supports the claim that the modality boundary does
  not transfer.

Neither is fraud and both may be defensible, but neither is currently *stated*. Either pre-register the cell, or
report all of them, or apply the multiplicity control this project already owns: `~/model_night/ensemble/`
(built 2026-09-18) picks on a dev half and reports on a disjoint test half, printing the dev→test drop as the
selection effect (52 cells, dev 2,214 films / test 2,215; best-by-dev 0.798 dev → 0.796 test). That machinery
exists and is used in exactly one benchmark and cited nowhere in the RSNA AI manuscript.

## 2. The detector number in the manuscript has no interval and an unjustified threshold

The quoted 90.6 % vertebra / 83.9 % sacrum come from `tools/or_lodo_rescore_outline.py` →
`or_ready/lodo/rescore_outline.json` **at conf 0.25**, which computes **no interval** and gives **no threshold
rationale**. The tool that does it properly — `tools/or_recall_curves.py`, case-cluster bootstrap, 2,000
resamples, full recall-vs-confidence curve, and it records `selected_on_this_test=false` — is not what the
manuscript quotes. **The same arm at its own best-F1 threshold reads sacrum 76.8 % [0.643, 0.821].**

That is a 7-point swing that depends entirely on a threshold nobody justified in print. Quote the curve, or quote
a pre-stated operating point, and give the interval either way.

## 3. One interval in 54 claims, and it is on the boundary

Of the 54 entries in `docs/RSNAI_CLAIMS.json`, exactly **one** is a CI bound (`carm_cv5_lat_whole_ci_lo`), and
the single `\ci{}{}` in `main_v2.tex` is that one. The bootstrap hits the boundary — [0.957, **1.000**] — so the
percentile method is the wrong instrument there; use BCa or a Wilson-type bound.

## 4. The plain-film CIs ignore clustering, and the quoted ones have no CI at all

`tools/head_mixed_length.py` computes Wilson intervals **on the vertebra**, which ignores clustering by film and
by patient — the superseded `main.tex` admits this in its own limitations. Worse: the two numbers `main_v2` quotes
(CSXA 99.87 % of 3,720; BUU 98.04 % of 2,698) are `per_source` subsets that carry **no `ci95` field at all**. Only
the pooled figure has one, and that pooled figure is from the leaked v2 bank.

## 5. Every comparison in the paper is unpaired eyeballing

`docs/RSNAI_PLAN.md` states the rule: *a "beats" claim needs the CI of the difference to exclude zero.* A grep
finds **no paired bootstrap and no CI-of-a-difference anywhere in `tools/`**. One paired implementation exists
(`tools/track5_mcnemar.py`) and nothing in the current result set uses it. Every comparative sentence is an
unpaired comparison on data that is in fact paired:

- render 71.7 % vs film 99.1 % on the **same** 233 bodies / 71 frames / 25 spines
- zero-shot lateral 96.3 % vs AP 37.9 % on the **same** cases
- card vs uncalibrated physics on the **same** folds
- pooled vs held-out detector on the **same** 56 frames

All four are one paired bootstrap away from being defensible. `tools/stats_compare.py` already has the machinery.

## 6. The device-card pass criterion has never been evaluated as written — and would fail

`docs/RSNAI_PLAN.md` criterion C3: *"gain > 0 on every device with the CI excluding 0."* Only the **median**
held-out gain is promoted to the ledger and the manuscript. The per-fold gains are stored and are **negative on
some folds**: Cios −0.0128, Elekta −0.0010, PIXIUM −0.0412 and −0.0203. The criterion as written has never been
run. Run it, and report the per-fold spread rather than the median alone — this project's own standing lesson is
that spread, not the median, is what catches a broken measurement.

## 7. Selective prediction exists, is good, and is invisible — and is computed on leaked data

`tools/level_selective.py` → `~/selective/SELECTIVE.md` (2026-09-15): risk–coverage with exact one-sided
Clopper–Pearson lower bounds, AUGRC 0.0466, a whole-run variant, and an explicit Jones-2021 caveat. 99 % selective
accuracy at 35.7 % coverage (n 2,956, lower bound 98.7 %); region-level 99 % at 55.7 %. **Cited nowhere** in
`RSNAI_PLAN.md`, `RSNAI_CLAIMS.json` or `main_v2.tex`. And it was run on the **v2 bank, which is leaked** — re-run
on the clean v8 bank before quoting any of it.

## 8. Abstention is live, untuned, and unreported

`tools/level_prototype.py:355` abstains when the decoded name disagrees with the single-crop name or p < 0.5. The
0.5 is a **fixed default, never tuned on a calibration split and never varied**. The C-arm scores carry the
consequence — lateral whole-frame answers 83.1 % of the time and is 98.4 % [0.962, 1.0] **on what it answers** —
but `main_v2.tex` reports none of it. A reader cannot currently tell that the quoted 98.2 % is at **full**
coverage while the deployed system also has an abstain path.

## What to do, in order

1. Re-run the detector number off `or_recall_curves.py` with its interval, and state the operating point and how
   it was chosen. Or report the curve.
2. Paired bootstrap for all four comparisons above, via `tools/stats_compare.py`; put the CI of the difference in
   the text.
3. Evaluate C3 as written, per device, per fold; report the spread.
4. Add intervals to the per-source film numbers, clustered by patient, not by vertebra.
5. Declare the multiplicity: say how many cells were computed and how the reported one was chosen, or use the
   dev/test split machinery in `~/model_night/ensemble/`.
6. Re-run selective prediction on the clean v8 bank and put the risk–coverage curve in the paper — it is one of
   the strongest things here and it is currently hidden.
7. Report the abstention operating point beside every accuracy, or say explicitly that the quoted figures are at
   full coverage.

# IDENTITY AUDIT LANDED (workflow `wf_cffe41e5-5ab`, 56 agents; 9 claims survived adversarial verification, 39 corrected)

Spec saved at **`docs/PATIENT_IDENTITY_SPEC.md`** (478 lines): resolution order L0–L9, per-corpus rules, 19 aliases
that must collapse, what remains unknown per corpus, failure behaviour, and a test plan with concrete
`same()` / `diff()` assertions. Read that file before touching `tools/patient_keys.py`. This section is the summary
and the consequences.

## D1 answered, mostly: a CTSpinoPelvic1K release id is NOT the same acquisition under two names

The feared case — one prone/supine pair filed as two release ids — is **ruled out** on disk: over 1,728 CT-quality
acquisitions, zero (origin, spacing, direction, nz) signatures are shared by two patients; 784 CTSpine1K COLONOG
masks give 784 distinct (shape, affine) signatures; the six cross-namespace geometry hits are all 1–2-slice
localisers at scanner-default geometry. TCIA's API lists 825 distinct PatientIds for the collection and
`patient_db.json` (built by `scripts/tcia_index.py` reading `(0010,0020)` off one DICOM per series) holds exactly
those 825. The release ships **`~/data/CTSpinoPelvic1K/manifest.json`** — 802 rows, `volume_id → token`, token
being the tail of the DICOM PatientID. That is the L1/L3 identity; the crosswalk regex was never necessary.

**Residual, honestly stated:** whether the 768 numeric-id records and the 34 `CTC-` records overlap as humans
(re-enrolment across the collection). One TCIA query or one email settles it. Everything checkable on disk says
no.

## D2 answered: TotalSegmentator v2 ↔ v3 is a RENUMBER, and `ts:s####` gets it wrong both ways

v201 `s1406…s1429` are **byte-identical** to v300 `s1463, s1504, s1641, s1725, s1736, s1775, s1785, s1806,
s1834, s1854, s1932, s1933, s1943, s1951, s1997, s2036, s2040, s2060, s2073, s2119, s2123, s2133, s2137, s2139`,
and the vacated v201 numbers were **reissued to different people** in v300. Keying on `s####` alone therefore
splits 24 people and merges 15. Both halves are live across `ts_drr` / `anchor_drr` / `physics_drr` (v201) and
`ts_drr_v3` / `anchor_drr_c2` (v300). Fix is a 24-row alias table (`tools/identity/ts_alias.json`) and a bank
registry that records the RELEASE, which no ann carries — 1,735 `ts_drr_v3` anns claim `TotalSegmentator_v201`.
Beyond the renumber, whether two different scans are one patient is **unknowable on disk** (no patient/accession
field in either release; age is jittered ±4 y between releases; institute letters remapped).

## THREE LIVE LEAKS, one of them in the bank I called clean

| where | what | size |
|---|---|---|
| **`allspine_v8_fov96_ctx`** — the "clean" rebuild | VerSe SERIES were keyed as subjects. `verse_demographics.csv` says 18 subjects own 37 series (verse400 = {090,155}, … verse417 = {277,278}). Five patients sit on both sides: verse401, 406, 407, 408, 416 — **367 of 3,502 VerSe validation entries (10.5 %)** against 269 training entries, and their labelled levels OVERLAP (verse214 labels 1–9 / verse261 labels 7–22) | 5 people, 10.5 % of VerSe val |
| **`anchor_drr`'s own train/val split** | `implant_<rel>_v<k>_<view>` is the same person as `ctsp_<rel>_<view>`; `make_anchor_drr.py:189` seeds the split with `random.Random(cid)` on the VARIANT-bearing stem, so one person gets three independent draws. **301 of 801 CTSP people on both sides. `anchor_det_v1` was trained on it** — that is the render-trained detector behind the 93.48 % own-domain recall (the 71.67 % real-film number is unaffected: real film was never in that split) | 301 people |
| **`vindrps_fov96_ctx`** | VinDr keyed at IMAGE level, not study: 10,466 images → 5,000 studies. **77.8 % of validation leaks** | 2,882 phantom people |

## The headline number has a caveat that must be stated

C-ArmLSpine: the five-fold CV behind **98.22 %** is split by `case`. The audit could not establish that 149 cases
are 149 people. De-identification left `PatientID` empty on 59,307 of 59,609 instances and UIDs regenerated per
file. `(0029,0016)` is distinct across 148 cases but five carry hand-typed bare integers ('8', '10', '4', '12',
'5'), so it is operator free text keying an **acquisition**, not a person. 149 cases fall on **four days** (66 on
2023-12-11, 43 on 12-12, 38 on 11-18, 1 on 11-12), median 224 s apart, console records created in batches of
5–12 within a minute. Ruled out: the same acquisition filed twice (all 296 frame PNGs and all 149 CBCTs distinct).
**Not ruled out: one patient, two acquisitions, two cases, two folds.** Conservative fallback available now:
**group the folds by acquisition day** and re-run. If the number holds, say so; if it moves, that is the number.
Also: `case134/ori/` holds 302 XA frames with no `.dcm` extension, a REAL `PatientID` and a real name, which a
`*.dcm` walk misses — handle and never print.

## Other findings that change code

- **Keys must be stored under `(root, case)`, not `case`.** The flat dict silently merged **161 case-name
  collisions** between banks.
- **`patient_id` is DEMOTED from first to sixth** in the resolution order. It is a bank-local string:
  `mendeley_drr` files a 35-year-old woman and a 64-year-old man under `mendeley_N24`
  (`tools/mendeley_to_render.py:296`, a bare `split('-')[0]`); `ctsp_drr_obl` holds 314 **VerSe** series named
  `CTSP_VERSE_gl279` that today's regex files as corpus `ctsp`; `spinalai` `sim_000237` is a nearest-neighbour
  index, not a person.
- **BUU across banks**: `films_ap_drr` (3,600 AP) and `films_drr` (3,603 LA) carry the same 3,600 patients;
  `films_ap_drr` is not among the keyer's roots, so the alias is unaudited. Add the root.
- **NHANES has a THIRD scheme**: `NHANESPS_C00160` / `NHANESPS_L00160` (nhanes_pseudo, 16,234 films, byte-identical
  to the raw tiffs) — 362 of 364 people. The alias is already written in `tools/filter_pseudolabels.py:39`.
- **DeepFluoro's fluoro `PatientID` is wrong**: splits 17-1882 into 2, 18-0725 into 3, 18-1109 into 4, gives
  17-1905 none, and MERGES 18-2799 with 18-2800 on one file (documented in the dataset Readme p. 13). Key by
  specimen, as the registered pipeline already does. The vendor-DICOM ↔ H5 join (D3) is possible by specimen +
  content, not by header.
- **FluoroCap**: document keying over-merges (one PMC id spans a woman with a IIA fracture and a man with a IB), video
  keying over-merges lectures and under-merges re-uploads. `patient_identifier_present = no` on all 1,616 rows.
  Accept over-merge as the safe direction and **never report a patient count**.
- **Byte-duplicates inside releases**: TS v300 `s1445`=`s1446` (and TS's own `meta.csv` puts them train/test),
  `s2107`=`s2109`=`s2110`; BUU `0140`/`0719`, `0449`/`0629`. None collapse today.

## What this does to the checklist

- **B3 done** — spec saved. **C3 is now fully specified**: implement L0–L9 per `docs/PATIENT_IDENTITY_SPEC.md` §1–2,
  key by `(root, case)`, add `films_ap_drr` and `nhanes_pseudo` as roots, ship `tools/identity/ctsp_token_map.json`
  (802 rows) and `tools/identity/ts_alias.json`, and the §6 test plan.
- **D1 mostly closed** (residual: one TCIA query). **D2 closed.** **D3**: join by specimen + content.
- **NEW A9 — re-run the C-ArmLSpine five-fold CV with folds grouped by acquisition day**, and state in the
  manuscript which grouping the quoted number uses.
- **NEW C11 — v8 is not clean.** Re-key VerSe by `verse_demographics.csv` subject (verse400–417) and rebuild the
  bank. Until then, every VerSe number from v8 carries a 10.5 % validation leak.
- **NEW C12 — `anchor_drr` split leak** (301 people via implant variants): fix `make_anchor_drr.py:189` to seed on
  the person, rebuild, retrain `anchor_det_v1`, and re-quote the own-domain recall.
- **NEW C13 — `vindrps_fov96_ctx`** 77.8 % validation leak: key VinDr by `study_id`, rebuild.

# SCHWINGNET — the focused manuscript, started the evening of 2026-09-22

Greg's directive, verbatim in substance: a new folder `SchwingNet/` holding an RSNA AI article that (1) takes its
introduction and motivation from the abandoned and partial manuscripts under `paper/` — no more internet research,
the motivation has been researched five times; (2) includes ONLY data from the rigorous approach; (3) follows
the CLAIM checklist, the RSNA AI author instructions, and the cai4cai RSNATemplate; (4) ships an ATLAS card for
the best model and one for the final dataset it was trained on; (5) removes ALL mention of the algebraic rule,
since it does not hold; (6) addresses every problem in the rigor audit; and (7) answers why DeepFluoro
contributes so much to the detector, and whether sources — including the CT-to-X-ray paired registrations —
should be weighted, expanded synthetically, or excluded on some measurable quality. He also asked: wasn't a data
audit going to be one of the rigor claims?

## Where it is being built

Workflow **`wf_0b84540b-455`** (task `w0x0zii0l`). Output tree `spinesurg-ct-nnunet/SchwingNet/`:

| path | what | built by |
|---|---|---|
| `sections/introduction.tex`, `sections/discussion_motivation.tex`, `sections/refs_used.bib`, `sections/SOURCES.md` | synthesis from `paper/rsnai/{main,main_v2,graveyard}.tex`, `paper/focused/`, `paper/qi_universal_protocol/`, `docs/research_*.json`, and `CTSpinoPelvic1K-1/paper/mpda/` | foundation `intro` |
| `results/rigor_fixes.json`, `results/RIGOR_FIXES.md`, `tools/rigor_fixes.py`, `slurm/rigor_fixes.sh` | A1–A7 and A9 computed on the grid, every number with a case-clustered interval and its source file | foundation `rigor` |
| `results/contribution_matrix.{json,md}`, `results/DATA_CONTRIBUTION.md`, `figures/fig_contribution.png` | held-out device × training set matrix from the `_1dev_`, A, B and P runs; marginal contributions; covariates tested (real-frame count, specimens, card R², typicality, pose coverage, manifold distance, registration quality); a weighting/expansion/exclusion proposal with one confirmatory job | foundation `contribution` |
| `CLAIM_CHECKLIST.md`, `claim_checklist.json` | all 42 CLAIM items → section + evidence file + verdict | foundation `claim` |
| `atlas/model.json`, `atlas/dataset.json`, `atlas/README.md`, `atlas/VALIDATION.md` | ATLAS 2025-11 schema cards | foundation `atlas` |
| edits to `README.md`, `paper/rsnai/main*.tex`, `tools/fig_row2_anatomy.py`, `tools/fig_row4_fluorohash.py` | the rule removed; the anatomy row now MARKS T13 instead | foundation `rule` |
| `main.tex`, `rsnastyle.tex`, `nejm.{bbx,cbx}`, `latexmkrc`, `refs.bib`, `figures/`, `tables/`, `README.md`, `NUMBERS.md`, `TEMPLATE_NOTES.md`, `FINAL_CHECK.md` | the manuscript in the template, every number traced in `NUMBERS.md`, limits checked | assemble + final check |

Each foundation was adversarially checked before assembly; the assembler was told to fix or exclude anything
that failed its check, never to paper over it.

## The rules the build was given (so a resumed session holds it to them)

- Only numbers with a case-level interval, a stated operating point, and a source file. Nothing from allspine
  v2/v6/v7, from v8 for VerSe, from `anchor_drr`, from `vindrps`, or any millimetre from `verse_drr` (A8).
- 3,000 words; 250-word structured abstract; summary statement; three key results; ≤6 figures of ≤4 panels
  (the six-row Figure 1 must be SPLIT); ≤4 tables; ≤35 references; abstention coverage beside every headline
  (A7); both C-ArmLSpine CV numbers if day-grouped folds move it (A9); the multiplicity stated in Methods (A2).
- Never the banned word (the French-derived word for where data came from); captions ≤60 words; numbers under ten spelled out; no commercial or regulatory naming.
- Template limits and the RSNA pages: both `pubs.rsna.org` pages returned 403 on 2026-09-22; the limits above
  are from the project memory `rsnai-author-instructions`. The cai4cai template and both ATLAS schemas were
  fetched from raw GitHub and pasted into the build prompts.

## What is already known about the DeepFluoro question, before the analysis lands

From `docs/MODEL_CARD.md` §4.6 and the run list under `~/spine-detector/runs/devcorpus/`: single-training-device
arms exist for all six ordered pairs (`dc_A_held_<X>_1dev_<Y>`), plus A/B designs with seed repeats and the
pooled run. The one scaling point on disk: with Elekta held out, training on Surgio alone gives 0.1221 vertebra
recall; adding Cios Fusion (the DeepFluoro real frames) gives 0.3024 — one point, one seed, no interval. With
Cios Fusion held out the detector reaches 0.916–0.940; with Surgio held out, 0.143–0.150. So DeepFluoro is both
the easiest device to transfer TO and, apparently, the most useful to transfer FROM. Candidate reasons the
analysis was told to test: it is the only source with 0.00 px reprojection registration; six specimens but 366
frames with a real pose per frame; a card with the best held-out R²; or simply that its image statistics sit
nearest the others on the manifold. Whether any of that is causal is what `DATA_CONTRIBUTION.md` must say.

(The F items for this section live in the checklist at the top of this file.)

# C11 IN FLIGHT — VerSe keyed by subject (21:20)

`tools/patient_keys.py` now reads `~/VerSeFusion/data/manifest/manifest.csv` (`series_id → patient_id`; 375
series, 355 patients, the 18 multi-series subjects verse400–417) and resolves a VerSe series to its subject by
whichever route produced the id — plain name, the physics render's `scan-` prefix, or the obl banks'
`CTSP_VERSE_<series>` patient_id, which the old regex filed as a CTSpinoPelvic1K patient (alias 8 fixed on the
way). Without a manifest a series stays a series: the collapse comes from the table, never a guess.
`tools/test_patient_keys.py` asserts it (verse090 = verse155 = verse:verse400; verse547 ≠; glgl003 = gl003), and
`allspine_v9_keys.sh` refuses to write keys if the test fails. Its log prints the v8→v9 diff: cases re-keyed
outside verse must be 0; the subjects that absorbed a series should be the 18.

Chain (`afterany`): keys 40415667 → bank 40415668 (~50 min) → train 40415669 (~2 h 40) → a5 40415670 (GPU).
Stage 4 runs `rigor_fixes.py a5-rows` then `a5` for each seed into `SchwingNet/results/v9_seed<s>/`, then
writes `SchwingNet/results/v9_a5_seeds.json`: point, percentile and BCa interval, Wilson on the effective n,
design effect, patients with an error — per source, per seed. That file is what replaces the v2 and v8 film
numbers in `main.tex` and `docs/RSNAI_CLAIMS.json`.

When it lands: re-run the leak check by patient key on the v9 bank (the `a5` step does it and prints
`val_patients_also_in_train`, which must be 0 for every source including verse_drr this time), then quote the
"overall" figure too — it becomes quotable once VerSe is clean.

- zero-shot (no C-ArmLSpine training): lateral 96.3 % (594/617), AP 37.9 % (239/631); paired difference

- detector, LODO C-ArmLSpine held out, operating point 0.74 FIXED BEFOREHAND on the other devices' sweeps:
  **80.5 %** [74.1, 86.8] vertebrae (231/287), **76.8 %** [64.3, 89.3] sacra (43/56). Replaces the 90.6 / 83.9
  at conf 0.25 that had no interval and no rationale (A1).
- render-trained vs film-trained on the same 233 bodies: +27.5 pp [20.5, 35.2] for film — but the film side is
  IN-SAMPLE (196 of 233 bodies were in the v7 train split), labelled so in Table 3.
- card vs uncalibrated physics: 24 folds, 4 negative; C3 holds on the percentile interval for all four devices,
  fails on BCa for cios_fusion (A6). Holm across the four comparisons: 0.002.

  cells exclude zero on BCa, none on percentile.

**DATA CONTRIBUTION — the answer to "why does DeepFluoro contribute so much" is that it doesn't.**
`results/contribution_matrix.json` (24 arms, 85 cells, recomputed from raw per-frame predictions at conf 0.25,
self-check 0.0000; `results/DATA_CONTRIBUTION.md` §0, corrections block on top is about stale prose, the JSON is
authoritative):
- Elekta/COBRA carries the detector. Elekta-only → held-out C-ArmLSpine 86.8 % [81.1, 91.8]. Adding Elekta to
  a DeepFluoro-only arm: **+71.8 pp [63.4, 79.6]**. Adding DeepFluoro to an Elekta arm: **−2.1 [−6.0, +1.4]**
  (seed 0), −6.3 [−9.8, −2.9] (seed 1).
- DeepFluoro is the carrier on exactly ONE target — Elekta held out (38.1 % alone; the model card's k=1→2 gain
  0.122→0.302 was DeepFluoro being added to a Surgio base).
- Mechanism: ANGULAR COVERAGE of real frames. DeepFluoro's frames all lie within 45° of AP; Surgio's are
  exactly AP and LAT; Elekta's span 0–90°. DeepFluoro-only finds the sacrum on 100 % of AP frames and 0 % of
  laterals; only Elekta-containing arms reach laterals (76–88 %).
- No pixel-statistics, card-quality or registration covariate explains the matrix; each predicts the wrong
  ranking somewhere (manifold overlap is symmetric, transfer is not; DeepFluoro has exact registration and the
  worst typicality, and is the best source on COBRA).
- Confirmatory arm: Elekta cut 2,640 frames/110 patients → 240/10, everything else fixed: 86.8 → 39.0 %
  (−47.7 pp [−53.7, −41.8]), the loss almost all on laterals (LAT 78.5 → 13.9); still +26.1 pp over
  DeepFluoro-only at equal size.
- SO: do not weight by card quality; add distinct frames at the MISSING ANGLES — real where they exist, styled
  renders under arc-spanning poses where they do not. That is the lever the parameterisation offers. The
  ×11 repetition arm that separates "more frames" from "a weight" is written and costed
  (`SchwingNet/slurm/contrib_eqcount_rep11.sh`, ~2 GPU-h), not submitted.
- Caveats from the checker: covariate correlations NOT quotable (a tool defect entered the equal-count arm with
  full covariates; fixed, not re-run); two jobs crashed after writing scores.json (cells real); Surgio card
  exists only on the grid; the pooled design P was not on the sheet.

**Figures**: this commit's main.tex uses the ASSEMBLER's five figures. Greg's six (workflow `wf_b0617df7-97f`)
are built in `SchwingNet/figures/`: fig2_pipeline MET, fig3_panorama MET (numeral 23 touches the skull; caption
mentions fields not drawn), fig4_confirm FAILED (a–c placed at 0.82× → 4.6–5.5 pt text; label collision),
fig6_arch FAILED (skip heads point into empty canvas; bowties for the BiGRU), tab_corpus MET (VinDr in-bank vs

**3. "wtf why are you still learning a 27-level posterior? is that necessary for vertebral detection?"** — NO.
The product is DETECTION + COUNTING from the anchor (memory: level-id-2d scope correction, 2026-09-22 earlier). The
27-level ResNet-18 + BiGRU classifier is the earlier "local level identity" line; it names from morphology. The
manuscript's headline 98.2 % is THAT model's number on C-ArmLSpine frames, not the counting chain's. The count is
an ORDINAL from the sacrum; the name follows by convention and, where it does not (L6, T13), the ordinal is still the
answer — which is Figure 1(a)'s point.
→ **A13. RE-CENTRE THE MANUSCRIPT ON DETECTION + COUNTING.** Primary endpoints: (i) per-body and sacrum recall on
real C-arm frames per device (LODO and pooled, case-clustered, fixed operating point — a1/a3 already); (ii) COUNTING
ACCURACY = ordinal from the sacrum correct, on real frames where the sacrum is detected, scored from the detector's
per-frame raw predictions against the CBCT labels (names → ordinals) — NOT YET COMPUTED; write
`tools/count_from_sacrum_eval.py` over `realxray_eval --dump-raw` outputs for C-ArmLSpine (LODO + pooled), DeepFluoro
and COBRA test splits, with case-cluster intervals; (iii) the sweep counting results where the sacrum is out of the
single frame (sweep_v3: CTSP 120 held-out 95 %, chains 100 % under the end rule; TS bench 522 unseen 98.5 %; films
21/25; DeepFluoro real sweeps sacrum 76.9 % over 6 specimens / 365 frames — `docs/RSNAI_CLAIMS.json` lstv_* and the
sweep docs). The level model leaves the key results, the abstract, the localiser half of Figure 1(c) and Table 2;
the abstention machinery (which rests on its posterior) goes with it. It may survive as ONE supplement sentence
("a morphology classifier was also trained; not used by the reported pipeline") with its clean-init number, or be
dropped. Figure 3 (naming curves) is therefore out; a counting-accuracy figure/table replaces it.

Consequence for A12: the re-initialisation still runs (so any supplement number is honest) but stops being
load-bearing.

Sequencing: the 2-fixes figure workflow (`w1ma7aetz`) must finish before any workflow edits main.tex again.
COBRA fetch launched independently (array job id in the log). The pivot is a workflow: counting evaluation
(grid) → Methods prose (all datasets) + Results re-centred + Figure 1(c) detector-only + Key Points/abstract/
summary rewritten → recompile → verify; then A10's retrain re-quotes the detector numbers when the full COBRA
set is in.

**Revision to objection 3 (00:05).** Greg: "if I can learn to classify vertebrae based on morphology that would be
a huge plus, I didn't think it would work ... that's not how the limited FOV localization is working is it?" It
is exactly how it works: the level model reads a 320-px context crop per detected body (target at 96 px, so the
neighbours are visible) and a BiGRU decodes the run of detected bodies. With one body in the run and no anchor
it names the exact level 72.9 % of the time (90.2 % within one), three bodies 92.9 %, five 97.0 %, whole frame
with the sacrum in the run 98.2 % (`SchwingNet/results/a4.json`). So the classifier is NOT removed; A13 is now
"frame the two mechanisms correctly", the k-in-field curve is the limited-FOV headline, counting accuracy from the
anchor is added beside it, and A12 (clean re-initialisation) is load-bearing again. The counting workflow already
launched (`w3stdna1e`) stands; the "re-centre" workflow was never launched.

tools/fig3_panorama.py curve constants A/C/S per region, chosen by a 576-profile search so the upright crop clears its

# THE THESIS, FIXED BY GREG (2026-09-23 00:20) — supersedes the "re-centre on detection + counting" idea

*"If I can just do limited FOV morphological classification the whole iterative stepwise process is essentially
unnecessary except for spines with an extra vertebra, so it serves as a second check, and since this is patients'
lives at stake it offers a safety net while the morphological classifier offers quick and cheap localization."*

Two mechanisms, complementary failure modes (memory `two-mechanisms-thesis`):
- **Morphology classifier = quick, cheap localisation.** One body, no anchor: 72.9 % exact, 90.2 % within one;
  three 92.9 %; five 97.0 %; whole frame 98.2 % (`results/a4.json`). One exposure, a name.
- **Count from the anchor = the safety net.** Names nothing, so an extra body cannot fool it: 45/54 transitional
  runs exact, 36 L6 (`RSNAI_CLAIMS` lstv_*). Its limit is detection (80.5 / 76.8 held-out).
- **The classifier's failure is precisely the extra vertebra**: L6 44.9 %, T13 ~0 %. That is why the net is
  necessary, not decorative. Errors differ (morphology vs geometry) → agreement carries information;
  **disagreement is the stop signal**.

- [ ] **A14. Agreement analysis** on the same real frames where the sacrum is detected: classifier name vs count
      ordinal→name; agreement rate; accuracy given agreement; how often the count catches a classifier error and
      vice versa; case-clustered intervals. Inputs: counting ordinals from `w3stdna1e` (`SchwingNet/results/
      counting.json` per frame/body) + classifier per-body names from `proto/cv_fold*/det/kfull` (and `proto_v9`
      once A12 lands). Run as soon as counting lands. Write `SchwingNet/results/agreement.{json,md}`.
- [ ] **A15. The net on anomalous spines**: the same comparison restricted to L6/T13 cases (renders where real
      transitional C-arm cases are absent; say so). This is the evidence for "safety net".
- [ ] **A16. Manuscript framing to match**: Key Points name both roles; Figure 3a is the limited-FOV headline;
      the sweep is presented as verification and as the only route when no anchor is in view; the Discussion says
      plainly that morphology cannot see an extra body and the count cannot see morphology, and that the system
      stops on disagreement.

A13 (frame the two mechanisms) stands; A12 (clean re-init) stays load-bearing.

# ERROR CORRECTION BY COUPLING THE CLASSIFIER AND THE ENUMERATOR (00:40, Greg's ask)

*"Can you create some sort of error-correction process, either a differentiable loss function or just rule based,
for correctly labelling T13, L6, etc. by coupling the classifier and the simple enumerator?"*

**The principled form, and it half-exists: ALPHABETS.** `tools/sweep_map.py::decode_alphabets` (line ~1435) scores
the alphabets ordinary / L6 / T13 / both (`ext` adds four-lumbar) against an anchored chain: each alphabet maps
ordinal n from the sacrum to a name; S(A) = mean_i log p_i(A(n_i)); the winner is the variant call; its margin over
ordinary is the confidence. The enumerator fixes the ordinals rigidly; the classifier votes on which alphabet the
run is written in; an extra body shows up as ORDINARY losing. Used only for chains; `level_prototype.py` (single
frames) does not call it — it has an embryo rule `count_vs_morphology` and abstains on missing sacrum / T12.

**Facts read 00:35:** per-body outputs keep only `top3` (no 27-vector) → the prototype needs a `--dump-posterior`
flag; the head's loss is one line of per-position CE (`head_mixed_length.py:155`); **C-ArmLSpine has THREE REAL
L6 PATIENTS — case42, case121, case136 (six frames)** — the safety net can be tested on real transitional spines.

**Workflow `alphabet-coupling` (launched 00:42):**
- Rule-based: `tools/alphabet_couple.py` — ordinals away from the detected sacrum; alphabet scores from the
  dumped posteriors; call, corrected names, margin, per-body disagreement (= the stop signal); no anchor → no
  correction, flagged. Self-test (ordinary / L6 shifted by one / one confident error / no sacrum). Evaluated on all
  five C-ArmLSpine folds (det + oracle, kfull + k5), on the three real L6 patients frame by frame, and the
  sweep_v3 variant calls on anomalous renders collected. Threshold set on train folds only. →
  `SchwingNet/results/coupling.{json,md}`. THIS SUBSUMES A14/A15.
- Differentiable: `--alphabet-loss λ` in `head_mixed_length.py` — CE over alphabets on runs with a known anchor,
  added to the per-position CE; heads retrained on v8 (v9 if ready) with λ ∈ {0, 0.3, 1.0}; film numbers must not
  degrade (patient-clustered a5); L6/T13 recall on validation-split anomalous renders. →
  `SchwingNet/results/alphabet_loss.{json,md}`. "Does not help" is a valid result.
- Verifier: extra synthetic cases (T13; both), a hand recount of one real L6 frame, no truth in the call, λ=0
  reproduces the shipped v8 numbers.

- [ ] **A17. Coupler results into the manuscript**: the count-vs-morphology agreement table (A14), the three real
      L6 patients (A15), and, if the alphabet loss helps, the retrained head as the reported one (then A12's
      re-init uses it).
